### Page 2

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2 
煤价格分别上涨 2.34%、2.69%；建材相关价格边际回升，熟料、全国水
泥均价和石油沥青价格环比分别上涨 7.90%、3.17%、9.09%，PVC 价格
上涨 3.09%，螺纹钢现货价格小幅上涨 0.36%。有色金属表现分化，铜价
环比上涨 0.27%，铝价下降 2.50%；新能源相关商品仍偏弱，碳酸锂价格
环比下降 22.33%，纯碱、光伏级多晶硅和热轧卷价格分别下降 2.92%、
0.60%、1.98%。终端食品价格整体偏弱，农产品批发价格 200 指数环比
下降 2.96%，猪肉批发价下降 3.64%，蔬菜和鸡蛋价格分别下降 8.53%、
12.57%；汽油、柴油市场价分别上涨 0.54%、5.08%。 
生产端结构分化，需求端仍受内需偏弱与假期扰动共同影响 。10 月，生
产端结构分化，长流程钢铁和部分化工行业偏弱，短流程钢铁、水泥发运
等指标边际改善；需求端受国庆假期影响较大，电影票房明显走强， 汽车
零售、快递和公路货运环比回落；地产销售同比继续走弱、二手房价格下
行，指向地产修复基础仍不牢固。价格端看，能源、化工和建材价格回
升，农产品价格继续下降，显示当前价格修复更多由上游工业品推动，尚
未形成向终端需求和消费价格的广泛传导。往后看，需重点观察假期扰动
消退后生产和物流等能否恢复，以及上游价格上涨能否在需求改善的配合
下形成更持续的价格修复。 
风险提示：国内稳增长政策推进不及预期， 地产市场修复不及预期，海外
需求超预期回落，能源及大宗商品价格波动超预期，地缘政治冲突加剧。

1. 生产端 
1.1 开工率 
截至 10 月第四周， 高炉开工率 （247 家） 为80.01%， 环比下降0.49 个百分点； 电
炉开工率为 64.10%，环比下降 0.99 个百分点；冷轧开工率为 83.02%，环比持平；螺
纹钢开工率为 35.73%， 环比下降2.75 个百分点； 磨机运转率为39.92%， 环比下降2.28
个百分点；浮法玻璃开工率为 66.75%，环比持平；石油沥青装置开工率为 23.90%，环
比上升 5.75 个百分点；PVC 开工率为 61.47%，环比下降 0.63 个百分点；纯碱开工率
为 66.55%，环比上升 2.89 个百分点；PTA 开工率为 69.01%，环比上升 2.04 个百分
点；全钢胎开工率为 57.79%，环比上升 0.16 个百分点；半钢胎开工率为 64.78%，环
比持平。

1.2 库存 
库存方面， 螺纹钢库存为147.21 万吨， 环比下降3.39%； 冷轧库存为129.50 万吨，
环比下降 0.99%；热卷库存为 360.40 万吨，环比下降 2.24%；铁矿石港口库存为
16,462.55 万吨，环比上升 0.14%；焦煤库存（247 家钢厂）为 761.14 万吨，环比下降
0.42%； 煤炭重点电厂库存可用天数为25.20 天， 环比增加9.57%； 水泥库容比为59.22%，
环比下降 0.22 个百分点； 水泥发运率为41.32%， 环比下降4.20 个百分点； 浮法玻璃库
存为 7,051.90 万重量箱，环比下降 0.72%；纯碱企业库存为 136.71 万吨，环比下降
7.88%；沥青仓库库存为 31,870 吨，环比持平。

2. 需求端 
2.1 地产 
截至 10 月第四周， 地产方面，30 城商品房成交面积为 172.90 万平方米， 环比增长
10.21%；100 城土地成交占地面积为 1,917.75 万平方米， 环比下降51.44%； 成交土地
溢价率为 1.39%，环比上升 0.72 个百分点。二手房挂牌量指数全国为 0.70，环比下降
6.64%；一线城市为 0.07，环比持平；二线城市为 0.44，环比下降 2.22%；三线城市为
1.01，环比下降 9.82%。二手房挂牌价指数全国为 139.72，环比上涨 0.04%； 一线城市
为 170.34， 环比下降0.27%； 二线城市为126.09， 环比上涨0.09%； 三线城市为120.72，
环比下降 0.01%。

投资评级说明 
投资建议的评级标准  评级 说明 
评级标准为报告发布日后的 6 个月内公司股价 （或行业
指数） 相对均值基准指数的相对市场表现。 其中A 股市
场以沪深 300 指数为基准； 新三板市场以三板成指 （针
对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针对做市转让标的）
为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准。 
股票评级 
买入 相对均值基准指数涨幅在 10%以上 
增持 相对均值基准指数涨幅在 5%~10%之间 
中性 相对均值基准指数涨幅在-5%~+5%之间 
减持 相对均值基准指数跌幅在 5%以上 
行业评级 
看好 相对表现优于市场 
中性 相对表现与市场持平 
看淡 相对表现弱于市场

### Page 2

世界経済：AI・防衛関連需要を受け景況感は好調。一方でインフレ圧力の広がりも確認
 9月のグローバル製造業PMIは53.0（8月52.3）と堅調。非製造業は54.5（8月53.6）と40か月ぶりの高水準
― 製造業はAI・防衛関連投資需要を受け生産、新規受注ともに上昇。非製造業も幅広い業種で業況が改善
― 一方、仕入・販売価格指数はともに上昇。エネルギーや輸送価格上昇によるインフレ圧力の広がりを示唆
 国・地域別では米国・ユーロ圏が製造業・非製造業ともに改善。とりわけ米国の好調が目立つ
― 米国ではAI関連支出の拡大に伴い機械・設備投資および生産が増加。非製造業においても情報通信業が突出して好調
― ユーロ圏でも、AI投資に支えられたIT関連サービスが特に力強く拡大。専門・商業サービスも改善
1
製造業の景況感（購買担当者景気指数） 非製造業の景況感（購買担当者景気指数）
（注） 日米欧：S&P Global、中：国家統計局。購買担当者景気指数：企業に対するアン
ケート調査結果から作成した指数。新規受注や生産高、雇用、出荷遅延などの指数

に一定のウエイトを掛けて算出。50が景気拡大・縮小の分岐点
（出所）S&P Global、中国国家統計局より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 3

金融市場概況：投資家心理が悪化し、日米とも株安・金利低下
 AIインフラ投資への信用不安の高まりから、日米株価はハイテク株を中心に下落
― 米新興クラウドサービスの社債価格急落が株安の契機に。原油高に伴うインフレ懸念も下押し圧力に
― 投資家心理が悪化するなかで、安全資産とされる日米国債に資金が流入、前月までの金利上昇基調は一巡
 ドル円は、1ドル＝150円台後半で方向感の乏しい展開
― 日米金利とも低下し、日米金利差は概ね横ばい圏で推移
― 原油高が輸入増を通じて円安要因となる一方、日米株安に伴うリスクオフや仏情勢悪化に伴うユーロ安は円高要因に
2

米国：インフレ率は高止まり、油価再加速で当面は物価高が続く懸念
 原油高や関税の影響から物価は加速
―9月のコアCPIは前月比＋0.3％（年率＋4.1％）と8月（前月比＋0.3％、年率＋3.5％）から伸びが拡大
― コアサービスでは住居費や輸送サービスなどが押し上げに寄与。コア財については、衣料品や玩具などが上昇。関税引
き上げに伴う価格転嫁が続いている模様
 足元で原油価格が再加速していることを踏まえれば、当面は物価の上振れリスクが残存する懸念
― シカゴ連銀が公表する週次の小売物価指標は9月後半から10月初めにかけて上昇しており、物価の高止まりを示唆
3

米国：足元の消費はガソリン高や関税に伴う物価上昇で足踏みも、年末商戦は堅調な見通し
 消費は足踏み。ガソリン価格高騰や関税に伴う物価高が家計の購買力を下押し
―9月の実質小売売上高は前月比▲0.5％と減少。ガソリン小売が減少したほか、自動車や衣料品も低調に推移
 物価高は逆風も、2026年の年末商戦は堅調予想
― 米マスターカードは、実質小売売上高（除くガソリン・自動車）は前年比＋2.7％と、前年（同＋2.1％）から伸びが拡大する
と予想。雇用環境の改善などを背景に幅広い所得層で安定した消費がみられると指摘

米国：雇用は減速も、AI関連需要が下支え
 9月の非農業部門雇用者数は前月差＋2.9万人と市場予想（同＋9.0万人）を下回る結果。7月・8月も下方修正
― 民間部門が前月差＋4.6万人（8月同＋8.9万人）と増勢が鈍化したほか、政府部門が同▲1.7万人と再びマイナス転化
― 民間部門では、旺盛なAI投資需要などを背景に、製造や建設、建築・エンジニアリングなどが増加基調を維持したもの
の、情報、金融、専門サービスなどの高賃金セクターが減少
― 時間当たり賃金の伸びは前月比＋0.1％（8月同＋0.3％）と鈍化。こちらも市場予想（同＋0.3％）を下回る結果
― 総じて冴えない結果ながら、3カ月平均では＋5万人程度を維持しており、雇用は減速しつつも失速には至らず

米国：対人型業種の雇用に底堅さ。労働参加率上昇で失業率は小幅上昇
 対人接触度の高い業種では雇用に底堅さ、AIによる代替が進みにくい可能性
― ヘルスケアや建設、飲食サービスなど、対人接触機会の多い業種では雇用が増加する一方、経理・会計サービス、ITシ
ステム開発などでは低調
 失業率は4.2％と小幅悪化も引き続き低水準を維持
― 主に16-24歳の若年層およびプライムエイジ層の労働参加率の上昇が押し上げに寄与した模様
6
（注）1.バブルの大きさは各産業の雇用者数
2. 対人接触度は、O*NETの職業別のPhysical Proximity（業務上、他者と物理的に近
接する度合い）を、各産業の職業別雇用者構成で加重平均した指数
3.コロナ禍からの雇用回復の影響を除くため、雇用者数の変化率は2023年末を起点
に算出

米国：生産は底堅く推移。AI・防衛が投資財生産をけん引、半導体増産も寄与
 2026年8月の鉱工業生産は前年比＋1.4％と底堅い結果
― 半導体関連の生産が引き続きけん引。生産能力が2022年対比1.7倍に拡大するなど生産体制の増強が背景。事実、主
要半導体メーカーによる工場新設・増強を受け、MOSロジック（GPU、CPUなど演算用）の量産が本格化
 AI・防衛分野を中心に投資財生産も堅調さを維持
― 投資関連財はAI・防衛が押し上げる形で同＋4.7％と増勢継続。国防・宇宙関連設備は同＋6.1％ と全体を上回る伸び。
地政学リスクの高まりを背景に軍用機やミサイル・防空システムなどの生産体制強化が図られている模様

米国：中間選挙を目前に住宅市場は軟調、アフォーダビリティへの不満が共和党への逆風に
 住宅ローン金利は足元で再び上昇
― 借入コストの負担増により、足元の住宅ローン申請件数は減少
 物価上昇が生活コストを押し上げるなか、金利上昇による住宅取得環境の悪化も家計の不満を助長する要因に
― トランプ大統領の生活費への政策対応は約7割が「期待より悪い」と評価しており、支持率は低下基調
―11月に控える中間選挙では、アフォーダビリティへの対応が引き続き主要な論点

欧州：G7は原油・ディーゼルの協調放出で合意。米国の輸出制限リスクは後退
9
 G7はIEAを通じ4カ月で1億バレルの石油備蓄放出に合意。初期20日間はディーゼルを前倒し放出
― 背景にディーゼル価格高騰。中間選挙を控えたトランプ大統領は、禁輸も辞さない姿勢を強調（米国は世界需要の4.9％
を供給）。米国からの輸出が欧州の需要を支える一方、米国よりも潤沢な在庫を保有する欧州に圧力をかけた格好
― 欧州は石油製品消費に占めるディーゼル比率が高く、価格高騰を回避する思惑も協調放出の合意を後押し
― 合意ではG7域内でのエネルギー輸出制限回避も確認され、米国によるEU向け禁輸リスクはひとまず後退
石油製品の製品別消費割合の国際比較
（出所）Energy Institute より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 11

欧州：ディーゼル需給逼迫は残存。欧州のインフレ上振れ要因に
10
 ロシアのディーゼル輸出は足元で急減。中東からの輸出減、中国の輸出停止も重なり、世界需要の約6％が失われた格好
―G7の1億バレル放出で当面の供給不安は緩和するものの、構造的な供給不足の解消には至らず
―IEAも、中東からの原油輸出は回復する一方、石油製品の供給制約はなお深刻と指摘。ディーゼル需給の逼迫が続け
ば、輸送・物流コストを通じたインフレ圧力が残存
 欧州では9月HICPが再加速。ディーゼル高が輸送・物流コストを通じて追加的なインフレ上振れ要因となるリスク
―9月消費者物価（HICP）は前年比＋3.8％（前月：同＋3.2％）と伸び加速。エネルギー価格の上昇が主因
今般の備蓄放出量と停止中のディーゼル輸出
（注） 世界需要およびロシア・中国の輸出量は2025年実績。中東は2026年8月実績を参
考に、輸出量を前年の4分の1としてシェアを概算

欧州：EU・中国はハイブリッド車の輸入制限で暫定合意。同様の措置は他の品目に広がる可能性
11
 EUと中国は、貿易不均衡の是正措置について暫定合意。中国からのハイブリッド車輸入は従来見通しの半分以下に
―EUが中国産EVに追加関税を賦課した2024年以降、追加関税の対象とならないハイブリッド車輸入が急増
― 欧州委員会の試算では、今回の措置によって2030年までの4年間で数百万台の輸入を削減見込み。直近では、EUが輸
入するハイブリッド車の約半分を中国車が占めており、域内自動車市場に一定のインパクト
 欧州委員会は、幅広い産業で貿易不均衡を是正する必要性を示唆。同様の措置が他の品目にも広がる可能性
― 欧州委員会のモニタリング対象（輸入増加と価格下落が併存する品目）は、中国からの輸入品が最多

欧州：2027年度予算審議が本格化。生活苦によるデモが拡大する中、財政赤字は高水準
12

欧州：長期金利上昇を受け、一部ECB高官は追加利上げの必要性低下を指摘
13
 フランスでは国内政治・財政面の不安から長期金利が上昇。イタリア・ギリシャなど周辺の高債務国にも波及
― フランス国債の対独利回り差は急拡大し、一時欧州債務危機（2012年）以来の水準に。その後、大統領選の有力候補と
目されるルペン氏（国民連合）が財政赤字を縮小する意欲を示し、拡大はひとまず一服
―ECBによる介入（国債買い入れ等）の可能性については、複数の高官が検討段階にないとの見方を示す
 長期金利の上昇は需要を抑制。ECB高官からは追加利上げの必要性が低下するとの見方も
― エネルギー高によるインフレ圧力と金利上昇による景気下押し圧力が併存。ECBの政策判断は一段と難しくなる可能性

 7～9月期の実質GDP成長率は前年比＋4.5％。1～9月の累計で同＋4.6％と、通年目標「＋4.5～5.0％」の範囲内
―7～9月期は、純輸出が＋1.8％Ptと成長をけん引する一方、総資本形成は＋0.6％Ptまで縮小。内需の弱さ鮮明

中国：政府は住宅ローンの利子補給で市場テコ入れ。人民元相場は緩やかな元高が継続
 中国政府が9/28、新規住宅ローン金利の年率1％分を補助する政策を発表。低迷が続く不動産市場をテコ入れ
― 対象物件の購入金額を150万元（3,500万円）以下に限定し、低中所得者層の住宅ローン負担を軽減する
― これにより、余剰在庫に苦しむ地方都市での実需を掘り起こし、市況のさらなる悪化に歯止めをかけたい考え
 人民元相場は、中国の貿易黒字が過去最高水準を記録する中、緩やかに元高が進行し1ドル＝6.6元台に突入
― 高水準の貿易黒字を背景とした人民元買いの実需が強いことから、当面は緩やかな元高が続く見込み
― ただ、米FRBの利上げで米中金利差が拡大しており、さらなる拡大が意識されれば元安に転じる可能性も

日本：旺盛なAI関連需要が製造業の景況感を押し上げ。利益計画は大幅上方修正
16
 大企業の業況判断は製造業が6四半期連続の改善。非製造業は悪化も一時的な悪天候が主因で変調の懸念は低い
― 業種別では、化学、石油・石炭製品、生産用機械等が改善。旺盛なAI関連需要や原油の供給不足懸念の後退等が背景

― とりわけ大企業・製造業では、輸出向け売上高の上方修正が顕著。上期の実績が大幅に上振れたことを反映したもの
― 世界的なAI関連需要の好調持続が見込まれるもと、下期も堅調な輸出が続けば、利益計画は一段の上方修正余地

日本：7月以降の設備投資需要はAI関連産業を中心に旺盛
17
 7～9月期の機械受注は増勢回復へ。AI関連業種からの受注がけん引
― ①旺盛なAI需要のもと、関連業種では設備不足感が強まっていたこと、②輸出増が持続するもとで、好業績が期待され
ること、③中東情勢の不確実性が後退したことなどが背景
 先行き設備投資は緩やかな増勢を辿る見込み
― 半導体製造装置をはじめ、AI・データセンター関連品目を中心に受注残が増加しており、目先こうした傾向が続く見通し
― 積み上がった受注残を段階的に消化することで、設備投資が持続的に下支えされる公算が大きい

日本：コメ価格下落で企業物価は鈍化。もっとも、先行きは原油高を背景に再加速へ
18
 9月の国内企業物価はコメ価格の下落を主因に、前年比＋6.8％（前月：同＋7.6％）と鈍化
― 新米価格が昨年を下回ったこと等を背景に、コメ価格が大幅に下落。国内企業物価を▲0.75%Pt押し下げ
― 一方、3月来の原油高を背景に化学・プラスチック製品は高止まり。また、AI需要等を背景に非鉄金属も高い伸びを維持
 7月以降の原油高再燃を背景に、先行きの国内企業物価は再加速する見込み
― 原油価格高止まりにタンカー運賃の急騰が重なり、先行きの輸入物価は原油関連を中心に加速へ
― 国内企業物価は石油・石炭製品やラグを伴いつつ化学・プラスチック製品などを中心に再加速する見込み

米国債券市場：インフレ加速懸念で上昇後、AI関連株の下落を受け金利は低下
 10月の米10年国債利回りは、一時5.3％台まで上昇したものの、足元では5.0％前後まで低下
― 月初は、原油高等によるインフレ加速懸念を受け、長期金利は約24年ぶりの高水準となる5.3％台まで上昇
― 中旬以降は、AI関連株の先行きに対する慎重な見方から、安全資産である米国債への需要が拡大し、長期金利は低下

く可能性

国内債券・為替：株安を背景に長期金利は低下。ドル円は150円台後半で横ばい圏
 長期金利は、株安を背景に低下。10年金利・30年金利とも、10月上旬から30bps以上の大幅低下
― 米新興クラウドサービスの社債価格急落が株安の契機となり、安全資産とされる日本国債に資金が流入
― 先行き、株式市場の混乱が続けば、短期的には一段の金利低下余地も。もっとも、日銀は四半期に一度のペースで利上
げを続けるとみられ、混乱一巡後の円金利は、再び緩やかな上昇基調に転じる見通し
 ドル円は150円台後半で方向感に欠ける展開。先行きも、150円台を中心とした円安水準が続く見込み
― 原油高は円安要因の一方、株安に伴うリスクオフや仏情勢悪化を受けたユーロ安は円高要因。目先は両者が綱引きへ

― 企業業績への期待から、10月初の日米株は上昇。S&P500は一時最高値を更新。日経平均も一時7万円台を回復
― その後は、米新興クラウドサービスの社債価格の急落を受け、AI・半導体関連株を中心に下落。原油価格の上昇に伴

― 金利が高水準で推移する中、AIインフラの過剰投資懸念が強まり株価を下押しするリスクには留意が必要

もたらす変化や、経済・金融市場の行方を展望

支えられ、世界経済は3％台の成⾧ペースを維持
【ポイント②】
世界はインフレ・高金利の新常態へ。成⾧・負担の偏在が経済主体間の
「耐性格差」を拡大

海外主要中銀はインフレ警戒から利上げモードへ、日銀も物価上振れを警戒

CHPT
Company description
Chunghwa Precision Test Tech (CHPT), established in 2005, is a global leader
in semiconductor test interfaces. Historically focused on the smartphone AP
segment, CHPT has successfully pivoted its strategic focus towards high-
growth AI/HPC applications. Utilizing its unique "All-in-House" vertical
integration model and leading mechanical design & simulation technology,
the company specializes in probe cards, probe card PCBs, and load boards for

top-tier chipmakers. This comprehensive portfolio effectively addresses
high-frequency signal bottlenecks, providing one-stop-shop solutions that
ensure structural reliability across the global AI computing supply chain.
Investment strategy
We rate CHPT at Buy/High Risk (1H). We believe CHPT is well positioned for
the AI infrastructure cycle, with leadership supported by its unique "All-in-
House" vertical integration model and broad participation across next-gen AI

GPU and US AI ASIC projects. Its technical high ground in advanced nodes
solutions and dedicated localized support provide it a defensible position, in
our view, as advanced packaging demand expands. The company captures a
wider share of the test interface value chain across probe PCBs, load boards,
and high-end burn-in boards, providing a multi-layered earnings engine.
Valuation
We value CHPT using a PE-based approach that factors in earnings
momentum and the structural shift towards high-end testing. Our target

price of NT$4,000 is derived from applying 35x to 2027E EPS, implying the
company's average forward PE since 2024 +1 SD, which we believe is justified
given the company's entrenched position in the 2nm/3nm testing segment,
rising contribution from US AI GPU & AI ASIC projects, and the unique
competitive advantage of its "All-in-House" vertical integration model. With
these structural drivers in place, and as we believe the market is starting to
value it as an AI/HPC play (rather than a consumer electronics stock), we

expect the multiple to hold at a premium relative to its historical averages.
Risks
Our quant system rates the CHPT stock as High Risk for its share price
volatility.
Key downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target
price, and to our earnings forecasts, include: 1) margin compression from
lower-than-expected utilization rates; 2) execution delays in concentrated
US AI GPU and ASIC programs; 3) intensifying competition in testing boards

from global peers; and 4) structural gross margin dilution from a shift towards
high-end burn-in boards.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

MediaTek  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 results preview – all eyes
on AI ASIC  development
MediaTek remains competitive in the AI ASIC service industry
given strong execution and design capability. We look forward to
3Q26 updates.
We expect US$2bn revenue of AI ASIC in 4Q26: MediaTek will host its 3Q26
earnings call on 10/30 Friday. It posted 10% Q/Q growth in preliminary 3Q26
revenue. Into 4Q26, given weak smartphone and consumer (eg. TV) demand, we

expect non-AI revenue to decline 10%-20% Q/Q. That said, thanks to US$2bn of TPU
v8t (ZebraFish) mass production, we expect 4Q26 revenue to grow 30%-40% Q/Q.
For gross margin, we expect 4Q26 to decline to 44-45%, as AI ASIC will likely dilute
the corporate margin.  Please see Exhibit 1   for our earnings preview.
Key focus for the call – all about AI ASIC (see next section for 2028 AI ASIC):
1. 2027 AI ASIC TAM and MediaTek’s market share: We expect MediaTek to

modestly raise its 2027 AI ASIC TAM or market share outlook, given better visibility
on TPU v8t (ZebraFish) CoWoS and substrate supply. Our  Asia supply chain checks
suggest 180k of CoWoS capacity could be allocated to TPU v8t, supporting
production of  3.6mn units, of which we assume 3mn units will be recognized as
MediaTek revenue in 2027. If substrate availability improves, we believe TSMC’s
CoWoS allocation to the TPU v8t could increase to 200k-220k units.

2. Google TPU v9 status (covered by Brian Nowak): This project is MediaTek's major
2028 revenue driver, and where investors appeared concerned, given uncertainty
around Intel (covered by Joe Moore) EMIB-T substrate supply. We expect Mediatek
to share that the TPU v9 (HumuFish) project is roughly on schedule for early 2027
tape-out, with high-volume production starting in early 2028, as previously guided.
We previously discussed the possibility of MediaTek using TSMC's CoWoS to help

with any 2028 TPU production concerns, and TSMC management indicated they
would be willing to help customers to smooth production if they were struggling
with EMIB-T supply. If MediaTek did want to tape out another CoWoS packaging
version of TPU v9, we believe it could take around a year based on existing norms.
Whether MediaTek adopts CoWoS for TPU v9, or waits for the next generation
(IceFish, or TPU v10) as a back-up, it currently is only using EMIB-T.

3. Google TPU v10 competition: As we described in Google ASIC, who gets what
for design services?, we see GUC and Alchip as partners for MediaTek coordinating
around the TPU v10 IceFish design service.
4. Second customer win? In our Nvidia partnership note, we saw a higher chance of
MediaTek winning a second or even third CSP customer (MediaTek had indicated it
was engaging with some customers, Aug. 31 interview). MediaTek may also consider
China CSP projects if its 3nm wafer allocation is sufficient for 2027-28, and projects

Morgan Stanley appreciates your support in
the 2027 Extel Asia Research Survey. Voting
opens 10 November 2026.

1. Substrate supply is the key, not demand, for MediaTek
production for Google TPUs
TSMC CoWoS could   support 2nm (Humufish) MediaTek's production for TPUs in 2028:

In our earlier report, MediaTek: 2Q26 results preview: Smartphone weak, but Cloud AI
taking off; OW (26 Jul 2026), we noted that, in our view,  MediaTek might secure a portion
of CoWoS capacity for 2nm TPUs to ensure minimum volume production. However, we
believe, in-line with MediaTek's intention, it will use EMIB-T from Intel  as the primary
solution to reduce costs and scale volume in 2028, if execution proceeds well.
During TSMC's earlier earnings call, TSMC management shared that it was willing to help

customers that may be struggling with Intel EMIB-T supply to smooth production, which
suggested to us that it would be willing to offer support even if CoWoS were the back-up/
second source. However, our recent industry checks suggest that MediaTek may not need
to adopt TSMC CoWoS for its TPU v9 as the EMIB-T substrate (for v9) output is tracking
its plan. If MediaTek did decide to use CoWoS, we believe the tape-out of the CoWoS
version of TPU v9 could take a year, based on previous norms.

CoWoS constraints eased, but ABF substrate supply still the gating factor for TPU
production bull case: In our recent AI supply chain tracker, we noted that MediaTek’s
180k CoWoS-S bookings imply ~3.6 units of TPU 8t (ZebraFish) in 2027, above our original

3mn shipment assumption. However, we continue to bake in some conservatism as we had
assumed ABF substrate shortages. So,  we still forecast 3mn units in 2027.

2. Could MediaTek add second and third AI ASIC customers?
Through the NVLink Fusion platform, MediaTek's XPU customers can leverage NVIDIA's
(covered by Joe Moore) scale-up and scale-out networking technologies to build AI server

systems. Our Asia supply chain checks suggest MediaTek may target subsystem builds for
a second CSP customer rather than supplying the XPU itself. To account for the impact of
the NVLink Fusion platform, we now assume an initial revenue contribution in 2028,
based on approximately 100k subsystem units at an ASP of US$25k, which is a rough
estimate based on materials needed, including HBM.
We therefore assume a US$2.5bn revenue contribution in 2028, based on 100k units and

an ASP of US$25k, including HBM chip consignment, known-good dies (KGDs) for
compute chips, and chiplets.
On margin trends, management highlighted continued value creation for customers across
both silicon and packaging design. As a result, we expect ASICs to remain accretive to
operating margins. At the gross margin level, management indicated that margins vary by
project, potentially reflecting support for full-rack systems. We model 2nm TPU gross

margins for MediaTek at c.35% (vs. c.40% for 3nm TPUs) given the larger revenue scale.
We continue to expect operating margins of ~20–25%, above the smartphone business
unit's 15–20%.

3. Why could TSMC be motivated  to support MediaTek and
Google?
TSMC's CoWoS-L solution carries a structurally higher cost vs. EMIB due to its larger
reticle size, as we discussed in The Foundry Floorplan: TSMC preview update; CoWoS vs.
EMIB competition (14 Apr 2026). We think TSMC may want to address the growing
competition from Intel's EMIB-T, for which we estimate 40-45k wafer equivalent capacity

in 2028 based on our Asia supply chain checks, implying a 10-15% share of the 2.5D
packaging market.
For MediaTek to adopt TSMC's CoWoS-L as a second source alongside EMIB-T, the RDL
(redistribution layer) connection to the interposer requires tape-out of three additional
photomask layers. However, our Asia channel checks suggest that Google may be willing
to absorb the incremental costs associated with dual sourcing, which could allow
MediaTek to sustain its gross margin.

We think MediaTek may produce 3mn units for TPUv9 shipments in
2028 with support from Intel's EMIB-T
Based on Asia supply chain checks, average EMIB-T capacity is around 5kwpm in 2026,
which we expect to further scale to 15-20kwpm in 2027 and 40-45kwpm in 2028. All of
the capacity mentioned above is converted for 12-inch wafer equivalent for customers'

easier understanding compared  with TSMC's CoWoS capacity.
Although some small projects are ongoing, we still see MediaTek/Google Humufish
consuming most of the capacities during 2027-28. We now expect MediaTek to produce
3mn units of TPUv9 (Humufish) with support from Intel's EMIB-T from late 2027-2028.
We see the current average EMIB-M capacity at 110kwpm, based on supply chain checks,
which could slightly decrease to 95kwpm in 2027 as Intel is converting some capacities

into EMIB-T.

Relative valuation
We view the P/E as relatively attractive – even after the share price rally – given strong AI
demand. Although MediaTek faces smartphone headwinds in 2026, we believe it is time to
look beyond 2026. We think this margin pressure is fully priced in, while TPU upside is
not. We believe the stock should trade more than +1SD above its historical average,
supported by strong TPU demand. Our  price target of NT$6,188 implies 42x  2027 EPS, on

our estimates.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
MediaTek (2454.TW)
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions: cost of equity 9.2%, intermediate growth
rate 12.0%, terminal growth rate 3.0%.
Risks to Upside
n Edge AI proliferates smartphone replacement cycle.
n Smartphone demand rises in China and other EMs.
n New products attract high demand, resulting in market share gains.
n Google's TPU spurs demand.
Risks to Downside

n Smartphone demand deteriorates in China and other EMs.
n Competition heats up, resulting in pricing competition.
n New products attract low demand, causing market share loss.
n Margin dilution is more severe.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Greater China Technology Hardware  | Asia Pacific
Positive implications for
downstream tech hardware
companies from US
hyperscalers' 3Q26 results
We view US hyperscalers’ commentary and capex guidance as
positive for the Greater China supply chain. The next watch point
is the impact of hyperscalers' CY27 capex guidance on Greater
China tech hardware companies' C4Q26 earnings.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+
Howard Kao

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
Note: META, AMZN and GOOG are covered by

Brian Nowak. MSFT is covered by Adam Wood.
We view US hyperscaler commentary as positive for the Greater China supply
chain: The tone was uniformly positive, with demand still outstripping supply. With
CY26 nearly over, the focus was on CY27 capex. Although quantitative guidance was
not provided this quarter, US hyperscalers’ comments suggested that demand
would remain robust into CY27.
We stay OW on the Greater China supply chain: We like Wistron, a key L6 partner

to Nvidia and AMD, with new customers bringing more L10 (Lenovo and an AI lab)
and L11 AI server exposure (Amazon, Oracle). We also like Lenovo, a key general-
purpose server supplier to Microsoft and Oracle, but also a rising supplier of AI
servers, with ~US$54bn AI server pipeline as of C2Q26/F1Q27. We continue to like
Wiwynn, a key general-purpose server supplier to three hyperscalers (Microsoft,
Meta and Amazon) and a potential assembly partner for two new customers (a

hyperscaler and an AI lab). Wiwynn is also a key supplier of Trainium server racks, as
well as AMD Helios racks for Meta.  We like GCE and EMC/TUC as beneficiaries of
US hyperscalers’ demand for PCB and CCL in general-purpose and AI servers. We
like Unimicron and NYPCB, Greater China’s key ABF substrate suppliers for
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
MSFT lowered its CY26 capex guidance by US$5bn to US$170bn, reflecting
delays in third-party capacity deliveries rather than a demand issue.
MSFT also guided for capex to “accelerate” in C1H27, driven mainly by CPUs
(higher memory & storage costs), GPUs and delayed third-party capacity
deliveries.
META narrowed its CY26 capex guidance to US$135-145bn (from US$130-145bn)

and said it would keep investing “aggressively” into CY27.
GOOG maintained its CY26 capex guidance of US$195-205bn and reiterated that
CY27 capex will increase “significantly” y/y.
AMZN maintained its CY26 capex guidance of US$220bn and said CY27 capex
would be “meaningfully” higher than CY26.
November 2, 2026 02:07 AM GMT

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

研 究  報 告                
十銓(4967.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
深化 B2B 布局開啟成⻑動能 
 投資評價與建議 
2026年營運受惠記憶體價格上漲及產品組合優化，2027年隨著新廠投產、企
業級客戶導入及邊緣AI需求；若公司能維持穩定貨源並有效控制庫存成本，獲
利有望維持高檔。 
 重點評論分析 
受惠記憶體價格漲勢持續 ， 推升營收獲利 ：3Q26營收季增33.6%至101.4億元 ，
年增143.5%，受惠記憶體報價上漲、產品組合優化帶動毛利率上升至49.3%，
營業利益率44.5%，本期淨利36.18億元，稅後EPS 37.19元，優於預期。 
庫存調配、產品售價同步拉升，有望支撐獲利：終端市況需求呈現分化，其中，  
AI資料中心跟企業伺服器、邊緣運算等較高階需求強，約佔整體DRAM產出
70% ；另消費端換機需求受到零組件成本上漲壓抑整體需求， 惟高階電競超
頻與AI創作者在快速運算跟大容量需求下仍強。在原廠優先生產資源向HBM、
高階DDR5傾斜下，除了減少DDR4亦排擠其他應用市場產能，將使工控、網
通、⾞用等⻑週期性客戶，⾯臨缺料與成本上升壓⼒。尤其部分工控及網通應
用仍依賴DDR4。截3Q26季底庫存達172億元，其中，DRAM約佔75%、Flash
約25%，公司現⾏庫存策略係以伺服器、工控⻑單及高階B2C為主。時序進入
4Q26，公司雖並未給予財測，但基於庫存調配及產品結構持續優化下，推估
4Q26營收107.９億元，毛利率52.3%，稅後EPS 48.6元。 
 投資風險 
記憶體價格反轉與庫存跌價風險、終端需求疲軟

### Page 2

2 
| 營運概況 
受惠記憶體價格漲勢持續，推升營收獲利：3Q26 營收季增 33.6%至 101.4 億元，年增 143.5%，受惠記憶體報價
上漲、產品組合優化帶動毛利率上升至 49.3%，營業利益率 44.5%，本期淨利 36.18 億元，稅後 EPS 37.19 元，
優於預期。市場別部份，至前三季 B2B(企業暨工控應用)營收從去年同期的 24.6 上升至 64.6%，B2C(品牌暨通路)
則降至 35.40%。 
庫存調配、產品售價同步拉升，有望支撐獲利：終端市況需求呈現分化，其中，AI 資料中心跟企業伺服器、邊緣
運算等較高階需求強，約佔整體 DRAM 產出 70%；另消費端換機需求受到零組件成本上漲壓抑整體需求，惟高
階電競超頻與 AI 創作者在快速運算跟大容量需求下仍強。在原廠優先生產資源向 HBM、高階 DDR5 傾斜下，除
了減少 DDR4 亦排擠其他應用市場產能，將使工控、網通、⾞用等⻑週期性客戶，⾯臨缺料與成本上升壓⼒。尤
其部分工控及網通應用仍依賴 DDR4。截 3Q26 季底庫存達 172 億元，其中，DRAM 約佔 75%、Flash 約 25%，
在庫存量部份，DDR4 占比約 60.09%，DDR5 約 38.51%，主要較早布局 DDR4 庫存；公司現⾏庫存策略係以伺
服器 、 工控⻑單及高階 B2C 為主 。 進入 4Q26 ， 公司並未給予財測 ， 但基於庫存調配及產品結構優化下 ， 推估 4Q26
營收 107.9 億元，毛利率 52.3%，稅後 EPS 48.6 元。 
2027 年前無價格下行趨勢：展望 2027 年，管理階層表示除了三大原廠之外，包含中國系廠記憶體廠，目前產量
以及未來 1~3 年陸續新增的產量，預期整體供需平衡至少要至 2028 年下半年或以後，且 4Q26 至 2Q27 係為缺
貨最嚴峻時期，2027 年價格難以見到反轉。產能規劃方⾯，新北約千坪自動化新廠預計 1Q27 進⾏裝修及設備進
駐 ， 2Q27 完工投產 ， 整體產能預估增加約 50% 。 十銓將事業群分為企業暨工控應用(B2B) ， 以及品牌暨通路(B2C) ， 
B2B 以伺服器 、 IPC 工控及邊緣 AI 為主要拓展 ， 提供記憶體與儲存方案整合 、 客製韌體 、 技術支援及可靠度驗證 。
隨著 AI 推論由大型資料中心向邊緣端擴散，不同終端設備對溫度、功耗、可靠度需求多元，具備技術支援及客製
化能⼒的模組廠，有助提高客戶黏著度與產品附加價值。另在 B2C 業務方⾯，公司持續深耕 T-FORCE 電競及 T-
CREATE 創作者市場，同時，過去十年在消費性產品中，高階電競與創作者模組占比已超過七成；在消費者對高效
能、大容量及超頻產品仍有需求的情況下，有助降低低階 PC 需求疲弱衝擊。整體而言，2026 年營運受惠記憶體
價格上漲及 B2B 比重提高，2027 年隨著新廠投產、企業級客戶導入及邊緣 AI 需求；若能維持穩定貨源並有效控
制庫存成本，獲利有望維持高檔。預估 2027 年營收 436.8 億元，毛利率 46.1%，稅後 EPS 169.64 元，目標價
340 元(2 倍 PER*2027EPS(F))。

6515 穎崴(WinWay)

報告日期： 2026/11/03 研 究 員：翁浩軒  (Davy Wong)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 AI 晶片帶動高階測試需求暢旺。 
 產品組合轉佳加上探針自製比重提升有助毛利率回升。 
 2026 下半年及 2027 上半年新增產能，帶動今明年營收創高。

一. 3Q26 毛利率有望回升，EPS 27.63 元 
穎崴 3Q26 營收 45.56 億(QoQ+29.32%、YoY+152.58%)，營收再創
單季歷史新高 ， 受惠於 AI 晶片對 Socket 需求暢旺 ， 加上 Probe Card
拉貨挹助，預期毛利率在 Probe Card 比重降低下回升至 39.7%，費
用率降至 13%，稅後淨利 10.07 億(QoQ+49.8%、YoY+170.86%)，
EPS 27.63 元。

二、4Q26 營收季持平，預估 2026 年 EPS 95.86 元 
展望 4Q26，儘管 Socket 需求持續放量，但在營收基期已高，加上
Probe Card 案拉貨放緩下，預估營收將季持平，而毛利率在產品組
合優化加上探針自製比重提升下預估將升至 41.3% 。 預估穎崴 4Q26
營收 46.2 億(QoQ+1.4%、YoY+106.84%)，毛利率 41.3%，稅後淨利
11.08 億(QoQ+10.01%、YoY+129.20%)，EPS 30.4 元。預估 2026 年
營收 156.8 億(YoY+99.57%)，毛利率 40.49%，稅後淨利 34.86 億
(YoY+108.36%)，EPS 95.86 元。

三.  2027 年新產能挹助獲利創高，預估 EPS 187.56 元 
展望 2027 年，AI 對高階測試需求仍強勁，晶片製程複雜性增加，
將帶動 Probe Card、FT/SLT/Burn-in Socket 及 Thermal 解決方案需
求，穎崴為多家 AI 晶片廠供應商，營收獲利可望持續創高。客戶
別 而 言，美 系 GPU、CPU、TPU 對 Socket 需 求 持 續，CPU Probe Card
將持續貢獻穩。另外公司切入 CPO 後段測試，目前於客戶端驗證
中，隨著未來訊號將透過光做高速傳輸，穎崴 CPO 測試解決方案
有望成為中長線新的成長動能。公司 2027 年 Socket 及探針產能預
估較 2026 年初翻倍，且隨自製探針比重提升，有助毛利率改善。
預估穎崴 2027 年營收 266.23 億(YoY+69.78%)，毛利率 42.45%，稅
後淨利 68.36 億(YoY+96.12%)，EPS 187.56 元。

四.投資建議：買進，目標價 6000 元 
穎崴深耕半導體測試介面、探針卡、精密彈簧針及溫控模組技術
投資評等：買進

2 
研發多年，為高效能 IC 測試治具的半導體測試介面設備商，公司
在高頻高速同軸測試座市場具領先地位，且與多家全球 IC 設計領
導廠商合作密切，在客戶研發階段即參與測試解決方案之開發。
隨著 AI 需求帶動半導體先進封裝需求放量，高階晶片所需的測試
種類、測試時間、複雜度均提升，均使穎崴測試治具及探針卡出
貨成長。考量到 1)晶片測試需求長期成長、2)公司毛利率可望落
底回升、3)2027 年新產能挹助帶動公司獲利創高，目前評價偏低，
參考 2027 年 EPS 給予 32 倍本益比，目標價 6000 元，建議買進。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
穎崴成立於 2001 年 4 月，總部位於高雄楠梓加工出口區，主要從
事半導體測試治具(Test Socket)及探針卡(Probe Card)之設計、開發
及生產製造關鍵技術，從晶圓測試(CP)、最終測試(FT)、系統測試
(SLT)、老化測試(Burn-In)及溫控系統(Thermal Control System)，提
供各種高速、高頻、廣溫域的測試介面；主要客戶為全球高階 IC
設計公司及封測大廠，為提供高效能 IC 測試治具的專業半導體測
試介面製造商。公司產品包括半導體測試介面、精密彈簧針、探
針卡、溫控模組，2026 上半年之營收比重分別為：測試治具 64%、
探針卡 27%、接觸元件 6%、其他 3%。公司為全球第一大邏輯測
試座供應商，主要競爭對手為日韓歐美廠商，國內同業包括精測、
旺矽、雍智科技等。

二.產業趨勢概況  
1. AI 帶動半導體測試需求 
受惠於 AI / HPC 需求爆發 2.5D/3D 先進封裝、Chiplet 架構應用快
速導入市場，晶片複雜度提升且封裝面績增加下，晶片尺寸放大
且測試接腳數亦快速增加 ， 另外晶片大廠在 AI GPU 、 CPU 、 與 ASIC
迭代速度加快，均使晶片測試規格提升且測試時間增加，帶動最
終測試（FT）、系統級測試（SLT）與老化測試（Burn-in）耗材需
求需求長期成長。穎崴深耕半導體測試介面、探針卡、精密彈簧
針及溫控模組技術多年，為高效能 IC 測試治具的半導體測試介面
領導廠商。除了後段測試，因應先進封裝成本昂貴，若在封裝後
才發現晶片缺陷將面臨巨大報廢損失，因此半導體廠加強在晶圓
切割前即進行晶片測試， 確認為 KGD ， 穎崴近年亦跨入 Probe Card
領域，與探針卡大廠 Technoprobe 簽訂 MEMS 探針卡供貨合約，
亦使探針卡營收比重近兩年提升至 20~30%水準。

2. 半導體測試介面市場高速成長  
公司的產品提供測試時所需要的硬體介面，稱為測試治具(Test 
Socket)，是 Device 與測試板之間的一個介面，結構中含有重要的
接觸電性元件(Contact Element)：Spring Probe，Spring Probe 提供
Device 和測試板(Test Board)間的訊號傳遞 ， 用於 FT/SLT 之 Socket ，
隨著 AI 晶片使用更多的 SLT 測試，將帶動公司的 Hyper Socket、
Coaxial Socket 、 Thermal System 需求 。根據研調機構預估 ，SLT&SFT 
2026~2030 CAGR 高於 15%。

3. 穎崴走在技術前端，與客戶共同未來需求產品  
穎崴與多家全球 IC 設計領導廠商合作密切，在客戶研發階段即參
與測試解決方案之開發，直接受惠於先進封裝、矽光共同封裝等
趨勢。公司之同軸測試座（Coaxial Socket）為目前 AI/HPC 晶片的

主流測試介面，支援最高 224Gbps 數據傳輸速率及高熱功耗處理
能力; 新一代超導測試座 (HyperSocket)提供晶片更柔和的接觸介
面，能顯著提高測試穩定性，同時實現阻抗精準匹配，有效降低
串擾，並大幅提升耐電流能力，亦可延長探針使用壽命並降低更

亦提供多樣化測試座產品線，包括可耐受廣溫域及高功率的老化
測試座（Burn-in Socket） 與 PoP &塑膠測試座 。 共同封裝光學（CPO）

4. 高雄楠梓新廠自製針產能持續開出  
隨著半導體需求暢旺，晶片測試複雜度提升，高階測試產品拉貨
強勁，公司積極擴大產能，新增產能主要來自仁武租賃廠房及楠
梓廠區，仁武二廠已動土，總面積 6300 坪(為一廠的 6 倍)，預估
2Q27 開始量產 。 今明年新產能包括探針及 Test Socket ， 探針部分，
探針 1H26 600 萬針/月，2H26 達 900 萬針/月，1H27 將達 1400 萬針
/月，除了滿足客戶需求，規模放大有助平均成本下降，自製比重
增加亦有助毛利率提升，Socket 產能方面，1H26 產能 3.3K 組/月，
2H26 達 4.2K 組/月，1H27 達 7.2K 組/月。公司未來仍將依市場需
求持續擴產，預估至 2030 年產能逐年提升，公司在後段測試元件
產能將維持領導地位。

YoY+170.86%)，EPS 27.63 元。

2、4Q26 營收季持平，預估 2026 年 EPS 95.86 元

41.3%，稅後淨利 11.08 億(QoQ+10.01%、YoY+129.20%)，EPS 30.4
元。預估 2026 年營收 156.8 億(YoY+99.57%)，毛利率 40.49%，稅
後淨利 34.86 億(YoY+108.36%)，EPS 95.86 元。

展望 2027 年，AI 對高階測試需求仍強勁，晶片製程複雜性增加，
將帶動 Probe Card、FT/SLT/Burn-in Socket 及 Thermal 解決方案需
求 。 預估穎崴 2027 年營收 266.23 億(YoY+69.78%) ， 毛利率 42.45% ，
稅後淨利 68.36 億(YoY+96.12%)，EPS 187.56 元。

### Page 1

기아 (000270)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.15  
잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기

3Q26 Preview 
기아는 3Q26E 매출액 31.1조원(+8.2% YoY, -6.0% QoQ), 영업이익 2.2조원
(+53.1% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 7.2%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 
물량은 좋았으나, 아쉬운 믹스와 환율 
글로벌 도매 판매 84.7만대(+7.8% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 83.0만대
(+8.6%)를 기록했다. EV·HEV 확대에 따른 ASP 상승은 지속되고 있다. 전쟁으로 인
한 원자재 상승으로 3분기 재료비 부담은 전분기 대비 3,000억~4,000억원 증가할 
것으로 예상된다. EV 판매 비중은 2분기 13%에서 3분기 15%, 유럽은 35%에서 
43%로 확대되며 유럽 내 마진 희석은 불가피하다. 원화 강세에 따른 영업상 부담은 
판매보증충당부채 환입 등으로 상쇄돼 3분기 환율 영향은 제한적일 전망이다. 판매보
증충당부채 환입은 QoQ +5,000억원 수준으로 추정된다. 4분기부터 환율 효과는 부정
적일 것이며, 환율 10원 변동 시 연간 손익 민감도는 약 1,500억원 수준이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 165,000원으로 하향 
미국 조지아 공장의 텔루라이드 HEV(2.8만대)와 HMGMA의 스포티지 HEV(1.0만대) 
신규 생산으로 북미의 견조한 HEV 수요에 대응하며, 2027년 믹스 개선이 기대된다. 
유럽은 EV3·EV5로 점유율을 확대하는 가운데 유럽 내 할인율 하락과 EV 잔존가치 
회복으로 판매 여건이 개선되고 있다. 향후 IAA 규제에 따른 법인·플릿 혜택 제한은 
중국 업체의 원가 우위를 줄일 수 있을 전망이다. 
원화 약세 지속을 반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다.  
다만, 현재 주가는 12M Fwd P/E 기준 4.8배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 
수준, 저가 매수 기회라 판단된다. 현재 배당 수익률은 6% 수준으로, 상대적으로 높은 
배당 매력은 주가 하방을 지지할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr

### Page 1

(005930) 
삼성전자 
2026.09.29

반도체 손인준 
6154 / sij@eugenefn.com

긴 호흡으로 
 투자의견 STRONG BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 560,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 270,000원(9/28)

 삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익이 103.6조원(+16%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 메모리 가격 등 전반적인 반도체 업황에 대한 기존 가정은 대체로 
유지했으나, 최근 원화 강세에 따라 원/달러 환율 가정을 1,440원에서 
1,400원으로 낮추고 MX 사업부의 적자 확대를 반영해 실적 추정치를 하
향 조정했다. 또한 2Q26 재고가 3Q26 매출로 인식되는 과정에서 발생하
는 추가 성과급 충당금 부담도 반영했다.  
 DS 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 104.0조원(+17%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 서버 DRAM과 eSSD 중심의 가격 인상이 이어지며, DRAM과 
NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +17%, +18%QoQ 상승할 것으로 추정한
다. 1cnm 수율 개선과 HBM4의 빠른 램프업도 실적 개선에 기여할 전망이
다. Foundry/LSI는 HBM4 베이스다이 생산 확대, Groq 3 LPU 양산 및 선
단 공정 가격 인상에 힘입어 하반기 매출 성장이 예상된다. 다만 성과급 충
당금과 감가상각비 부담으로 영업적자 축소 폭은 제한적일 것으로 보인다. 
 DX 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 -1.8조원(적자지속qoq)을 기록할 것으로 추
정한다. 삼성전자는 메모리 가격 급등에도 제품 가격 인상을 제한하며 시장 
점유율 확대에 주력하고 있다. 이러한 가격 정책과 갤럭시 Z 폴드8 등 신제
품 흥행에 힘입어 판매량은 당초 예상치를 상회할 것으로 보인다. 다만 원
가 상승분의 전가가 제한되면서 영업적자 폭은 크게 확대될 전망이다.  
 삼성전자의 4Q26 영업이익은 114.9조원(+11%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. DRAM과 NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +11%, +12%QoQ 상승
하며 견조한 흐름을 이어갈 것으로 보인다. 환율 가정 조정에 따라 2026년
과 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 각각 365.2조원(+735%yoy), 595.8조원
(+63%YoY)으로 낮췄다. 4Q26~1Q27 범용 DRAM·NAND 및 2027년 
HBM 가격 협상 환경은 공급업체에 유리하게 전개되고 있어, 향후 가격 가
정의 추가 상향 가능성이 높다고 판단한다.  
 극심한 DRAM 공급 부족으로 인한 HBM 적층 단수 하향 조정(base die 수
요 증가), Memory Wall에 대응하기 위한 3D Stacked DRAM·zHBM 도입 
논의 등은 파운드리의 기술적 가치를 부각시키는 요인이다. 파운드리의 수
익성 회복 속도는 완만할 전망이지만, 메모리와 파운드리를 연계한 턴키 비
즈니스의 시너지는 AI 반도체 시장 내 경쟁력을 높이고, 메모리 시장 점유
율 확대에도 기여할 것으로 보인다. 적극적인 메모리 가격 인상 정책으로 
업계 최고 수준의 Blended ASP도 장기간 유지할 것으로 예상한다. 
 이제 메모리 업종 주가는 업황의 지속 가능성을 확인하고 꾸준한 주주환원
을 반영하며, 중장기적으로 점진적인 상승세를 이어갈 것으로 판단한다. 삼
성전자는 메모리·파운드리를 아우르는 턴키 역량과 가격 정책에 기반한 실
적 차별화를 통해 피어 그룹 내 경쟁력을 높여갈 것으로 보인다.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

Document 282: Advanced economies _ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Advanced economies _ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
History tends to repeat itself in advanced economies. Once again, growth ultimately fell short of expectations by only a small
margin in the first half of 2026, despite the conflict in Iran. As early as 2025, the impact of tariffs was less severe than feared.
This is a sign that structural factors (notably AI, defence and electrification) are underpinning growth in these countries

and are expected to continue to do so in 2027. However, there are significant risks to the economic outlook, primarily of a
geopolitical nature, with a significant upside risk to inflation and interest rates. The other instance of history repeating itself
concerns a period that those under 20 cannot have experienced: the period leading up to the 2008 crisis, echoes of which can
be seen in terms of growth, inflation, the direction of monetary policy, and long-term interest rates.

Advanced economies are experiencing trends similar to those they
faced nearly 20 years ago:
- Growth remains resilient in 2025-26 despite shocks, as it did in 2006-
07, in the major advanced economies.
- Inflation is above central banks’ targets: as in 2006-07, this calls for a
tightening of monetary policy.
- In Europe and the United States, long-term interest rates have re -
turned to their 2006-07 levels (1996 in Japan): a challenging environ-

ment for public finances in a context where public debt ratios are signi-
ficantly higher than those seen 20 years ago.
THE GLOBAL ECONOMY IN THE MIDST OF A GROWTH CYCLE
We are already well into 2026, and the picture so far is clear: glo -
bal growth is proving resilient. Following a US tariff shock last year,
growth is once again proving, for the time being, to be significantly less
affected by the conflict in Iran than had been anticipated when it first

broke out. Initially, however, there were fears that this new shock would
hit growth even harder. As with the tariff hike in 2025, the impact was
measurable, particularly in terms of consumer confidence. But in both
2025 and 2026, growth has also shown a degree of resilience.
The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise
in oil prices, the recessionary impact of which is not yet apparent.
According to our forecasts, growth was expected to reach or exceed 1%

in 2026 in the four major advanced economies (the Eurozone, Japan,
the United Kingdom and the United States, where it exceeds 2%) for
the second consecutive year. This had not happened (excluding the
post-COVID catch-up period) since 2006-07. Growth figures are more
moderate (particularly for demographic reasons), but the private sec -
tor is not (at present) showing the same excesses (at that time, the
credit boom had preceded the worst crisis since the 1930s); although

debt levels have recently risen in the US tech sector.
The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial
intelligence (AI). It is fuelling global trade and business investment
in most countries. This is clearly the case in the United States, where
business investment is growing strongly as investment plans in the
US tech sector are implemented.
However, the world’s major economies are currently engaged in a
broader push to expand their capital goods investment. Alongside the

ongoing development of services—particularly digital services—we
have seen a rebound in industrial production in most countries since
early 2025. The leading sectors are primarily technology equipment,
machinery, aeronautics, defence and their essential inputs (energy and
metals). This trend is evident everywhere, including in Europe, and
is becoming entrenched over the long term, with an improvement in
business climate indicators, particularly new export orders. Growth is

therefore expected to strengthen further in 2027, particularly in the
Eurozone, where German investment plans are set to gather momen -
tum and whose spillover effects will have a greater impact on the eco-
nomy.
However, not all sectors are experiencing the same momentum. This
is particularly true for the sectors that depend on household consump-
tion, which has shown mixed trends. Our observation that consumer
spending is more buoyant in the United States than in Europe was

confirmed in 2026, and the outlook does not point to a change in this
ranking.
EDITORIAL
RESILIENCE TO SHOCK AFTER SHOCK

The bank
for a changing
world
While European and US households are experiencing more or less the
same energy price shock, inflation remains higher in the United States.
But this has not prevented consumer spending from keeping pace, pri-
marily because it is driven by the wealthiest households, who are less
sensitive to inflation than to the wealth effect. Furthermore, while the

savings rate remains high in the Eurozone, US households have been
drawing down their savings. This is also true of British households,
which explains why the UK has seen slightly stronger growth since
the beginning of the year. In Japan, government spending has partially
offset energy inflation (while other countries are doing so to a much
lesser extent than in 2022), which has supported consumption growth.
However, these buffers—reduced savings or fiscal support—have their

limits when shocks persist. The two major current conflicts (Iran and
Ukraine) are far from being resolved, and their impact is continuing.
This persistent geopolitical risk and its implications for inflation repre-
sent a significant headwind to the growth outlook for 2027.
HIGHER INFLATION CALLS FOR MONETARY TIGHTENING
Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks
examined in this publication, which is another similarity (albeit with

different underlying causes) with the 2006-07 period. Japan is the ex -
ception, as government support measures for households are tempo -
rarily holding back inflation.
Apart from the Bank of Japan, which is engaged in a long-term norma-
lisation process, no central bank was expected to raise its key rates
in 2026. But they are now being prompted to do so for reasons that
vary from one region to another. The first is the conflict in Iran and
the resulting rise in oil prices, which triggered a rebound in energy

inflation from the second quarter onwards. However, since the conflict
was not resolved by the memorandum of understanding signed in June,
and given that the subsequent drop in oil prices proved short-lived,
inflation is expected to rise again. The rise in gas prices is expected to
exacerbate the impact, particularly in Europe, where inventories are
being replenished ahead of winter. Electricity prices are expected to
follow suit in most countries, but at a more moderate pace than in

2022. Indeed, gas is now used less to generate electricity than it was
at that time (thanks to the expansion of renewable energy), and to an
even lesser extent in France.
There is a significant risk that energy inflation will spread further to
other components of inflation in the future, and inflation expectations
could eventually reflect this. This is why the European Central Bank
has already raised its key interest rate twice (in June and September)

and, according to our scenario, will likely do so again in December. For
the same reason, we expect the Bank of England to raise its policy rate
in November. Although the cap on energy prices was maintained for
a long time, it has now been raised. As a result, inflation is expected
to rise between now and the end of the year. The aim will then be to
prevent second-round effects.
Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength. In the

United States, it has exceeded the 2% target since 2021. The main
argument in favour of a new cycle of rate hikes (following the one in
2022-2023) lies in the current resilience of growth and the improving
labour market. The Federal Reserve (Fed) must balance two equally
important objectives: maximum employment and price stability. Howe-
ver, unlike in the 4 th quarter of 2025, when the decline in job crea -
tion justified monetary easing (by 75 basis points), the current sharp

rebound in job creation completely reverses that analysis. According
to our forecasts, the unemployment rate is expected to decrease in
the coming months, which pushes back the prospect of a decline in
core inflation. This situation persists due to both supply constraints
(over which a central bank has no control) and excess demand (which
the Fed can, however, mitigate). Consequently, the Fed raised its key
rate in September and is expected to implement two further rate hikes

between now and January 2027.
In Japan, finally, while headline inflation has been brought down by
government support measures, core inflation remains significant.
Wage pressures are mounting against a backdrop of labour shortages.
The Bank of Japan is therefore being forced to accelerate the pace of its
monetary normalisation.
In all these regions, inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026
or Q1 2027. It is against this backdrop of temporary inflation that rate

hikes could also come to an end by that time (excluding Japan). Howe-
ver, this assumes that once the energy shock—the course of which
remains uncertain—subsides, inflation will fall. The effects of El Niño,
which are still difficult to gauge at present, could take over. Inflation
would then remain above the central banks’ target for a longer period.
Under this scenario, rate hikes would go further than is currently en -
visaged.
THE RISE IN LONG- TERM INTEREST RATES: A FACTOR THAT WILL WEIGH

ON PUBLIC FINANCES
Monetary policies have also to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal
policy, particularly in the United States (where the budget deficit is
high and shows no signs of shrinking) and in Japan (where the new
government is pursuing pro-growth policies). In Europe, fiscal policy
is playing a much smaller role than it did in 2022. While the stimulus
measures implemented at that time did help reduce the inflation rate
in the short term (by nearly 2.5 pp in France, for example), by keeping

demand at a high level, they delayed disinflation and increased the
scale of the monetary tightening required.
The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: it triggered a
rise in long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the
start of the year) to levels close to those of 2006-07. This rise is wides-
pread, but its causes vary. Inflation expectations appear to play a more
significant role in the Eurozone, whereas the rise in real interest rates

seems to be the main factor in the United States and Japan.

The bank
for a changing
world
Higher inflation and more resilient real growth than feared point in
the same direction: stronger than expected nominal growth that is li -
kely to be sustained. However, in the long term, high nominal growth
tends to drive up nominal interest rates (as it is a positive sign of more
profitable investment projects), in contrast to the problem of secular

stagnation raised in the 2010s (which combined low nominal growth
with low nominal interest rates).
The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period
of growth), with various situations. In several countries, the public
debt-to-GDP ratio continues to rise. In the United States, deficit reduc-
tion does not appear to be a priority, to the extent that it is expected
to remain above 6% of GDP at the very least until the end of Donald

Trump’s term of office (with little hope that the mid-term elections will
significantly alter this trend). In the United Kingdom and France, fiscal
consolidation has begun, but it is being hampered by the simultaneous
increase in certain areas of expenditure (debt servicing, defence and
healthcare, due in particular to an ageing population), which is slowing
the pace of consolidation. In Germany, the implementation of invest -
ment plans is leading to a widening of the deficit. While this does not

pose a sustainability problem, it does result in an increase in annual
debt issuance. In Japan, the budget deficit is kept in check by interest
payments that remain low, as the rise in interest rates is recent and
the average maturity of the debt is high (10 years). However, expansio-
nary fiscal policy could pose further problems in terms of debt dyna -
mics if it were to continue through the end of the decade. In Italy and
Spain, fiscal consolidation has been sufficient to stabilise the public

debt-to-GDP ratio (and even to reduce it in Spain), but the high level of
Italy’s debt service it vulnerable to a rise in interest rates.
In all these countries, the fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it
will be in 2030. Indeed, in most cases, only half of the debt was is -
sued after 2022 (that is, after the end of the zero-interest-rate period).
The debt service has so far only partially risen, except in the United
States, where most of the increase has already occurred due to a shor-

ter average debt maturity (6 years). This means that, for all countries
combined, the rate of GDP growth remains higher than the average
interest rate paid on public debt (or the effective interest rate).
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with
GDP growth. As a result, in most cases, the debt service will have re -
turned to the level as a share of GDP seen in the early 2000s. In order to
stabilise public debt-to-GDP ratios, primary balances will need to have

improved. This will even require the generation of primary surpluses,
particularly in Italy (which is already achieving this), France and the
United Kingdom. This is a crucial shift that governments will need to
introduce as early as the 2027 budget; otherwise, they risk postponing
the stabilisation of their public debt-to-GDP ratio once again.
Article completed on 17 September 2026

Key points
• Total dwelling approvals rose 5.1%mth
in September, although the annual pace
moved into contraction at –0.8%yr with
the detail suggesting the underlying
cycle is starting to turn down.

• The headline increase was driven by
another volatile rise in private unit
approvals (+16.6%) which more than
offset a significant decline in private
detached house approvals (–5.3%).
• Units drove gains in NSW and
Queensland. WA, SA and Victoria all
recorded declines. Detached dwelling
approvals fell across all states.
• The total value of residential approvals
fell 2.8%mth but a sharp rise in non-
residential approvals – led by the
data centres – saw the total value of

building approvals rise 13%mth.
• Interest rate increases, weakness in
established housing markets, rising costs
and pessimistic consumer sentiment
are likely to remain headwinds.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Total dwelling approvals rose 5.1%mth in September, while
the annual pace moved into contraction at –0.8%yr. The
result was well above both Westpac’s expectation for a
7.0%mth decline and the market expectation for a 2.0%mth
rise. Unit approvals continued to drive headline volatility,

surging in September, while detached dwellings, which
provide a better read on the underlying trend, recorded a
sizeable decline.
Note that most figures in the charts and table are presented on
a rolling-three month basis to smooth out monthly volatility.
Private detached dwelling approvals fell 5.3%mth in
September, unwinding August’s upwardly-revised 5.4%mth
rise (+1.7ppts). Recent resilience has kept the segment
elevated, with approvals remaining above 10,000 and the

annual pace at 9.6%yr. However, the quarterly profile provides
a clearer read on the underlying trend and suggests detached
dwelling approvals are beginning to respond to recent interest
rate increases (see here). Approvals rose just 0.9%qtr in
September vs 3.1%qtr in June and 5.9%qtr in March.
Private unit approvals remained volatile, rising 16.6%mth in
September following 21.7%mth decline in August. Strength
was led by high-rise approvals, while low-mid-rise approvals

recorded a more modest increase. Despite the monthly rise,
the annual pace fell further into contraction at –20.5%yr.
Among the states, NSW recorded the strongest result,
with private approvals rising 35.3%mth led by a surge in
private unit approvals. Queensland also recorded an increase
(+9.3%mth), while WA (–2.9%mth), SA (–3.5%mth) and
Victoria (–29.0%mth) all declined.
In value terms, residential building approvals fell 2.8%mth,
driven by a 3.3%mth decline in new residential approvals,

partially offset by a 0.8%mth rise in renovation activity.
Non-residential approvals once again reversed sharply, rising
45.9%mth, as the data centre-dominated ‘other commercial’
segment recorded an 8-fold increase on a non-seasonally
adjusted basis. The total value of building approvals rose
13.0%mth in September. On a non-seasonally adjusted basis,
the value of building approvals rose 8.3%mth overall, but
declined 3.0%mth when excluding the ‘other commercial’

segment.
September’s result reinforces the importance of looking
through headline volatility in unit approvals. While total
approvals rose in the month, detached dwelling approvals
slowed on a quarterly basis and fell across all states, pointing
to softer underlying momentum. The annual pace of total
approvals also moved into contraction for the first time
since January. Interest rate increases, the sharp downturn
in established housing markets and rising costs are likely to

weigh on further growth, with pessimistic consumer sentiment
an additional headwind (see here).
Detached approvals point to softer momentum

ion

◼ 
 Sovereign risk
 : 
the government debt burden 
 is moderate and 
 will remain 
 so in the short term, even if the fiscal deficit is projected to 
deteriorate
 . The fiscal deficit could exceed 3% of GDP in 2026 due to support measures in response to the energy shock (estimated at 
less than 1% of GDP in H1 2026), but this will not endanger public
 -
 debt dynamics. Government debt should fall again in 2027 thanks 
to lower deficits and fast GDP growth. Debt was only 30% of GDP in 2025 (vs. 41% in 2019) and interest payments consumed less  
than 5% of fiscal revenue (vs. 7.2% in 2019). The government cover
 s its funding needs mostly in the local bond market, which is 
deepening gradually and not 
 highly 
 exposed to foreign investors. Government debt in foreign currency declined to about 25% of total 
debt in 2025 (vs. 38% in 2019); this is still substantial, but almost all this FC debt 
 is made up  
 of concessional loans from official 
creditors. On the negative front, high credit risks in the state corporate and bank sectors 
 are 
 very large contingent risks for the 
sovereign. Moreover, public investment needs related to climate change and infrastructure development will increase in the 
 medium 
term
.

◼ 
 Systemic credit risk
 : 
Domestic credit to the private sector is too large and still rising (141% of GDP in 2025 vs. 108% in 2019) while 
the banking sector remains poorly capitali
 s
ed. Credit risks and resource misallocation have long been exacerbated by opacity and 
supervision flaws, as well as the use of high credit growth targets to support economic growth (+15% in 2026). In 2022
 –
2023, banks 
faced severe funding stress and asset
 -
 quality deterioration resulting from the property and bond market crisis. The situation has 
recovered gradually since then. In the 
 short term
 , credit risks may increase amid higher energy costs and if  
there is  
a correction in 
the tech cycle. Some banks, especially small private ones, also remain exposed to VND funding risk (whereas banks do not rely  
much 
on 
 foreign
-
 currency 
 financing). Positively, reforms, including stricter capital
 -
 adequacy requirements, continue to be introduced 
gradually 
 in order 
 to improve banks’ creditworthiness (
 the 
 SBV plans to test the removal of credit quotas for the soundest banks 
from 2026).

Editorial 
Increasing signs of a bottom 
Strategy summary: Democrats’ thumping win in the US mid -
term elections has three key implications: i) policy gridlock is 
back in Washington, likely stalling fiscal expansion and new 
tariffs; ii) the chance of a prolonged Middle East conflict is 
curtailed, lowering oil prices and inflation expectations; and  iii) 
the pace of AI data-centre buildout is at risk , curbing AI-linked 
bond supply. Higher corporate and wealth taxes are a long-term 
risk if Democrats win the 2028 presidential elections.   
For portfolios, the election reinforce s our mid-cycle playbook 
already in place:  remaining broadly balanced across assets, 
while looking for tactical opportunities. As the S&P500 volatility 
index (VIX) surpasses 25, we see rising signs of equity market 
bottoming. We add an opportunistic idea on the US IT sector 
(see page 4), while using elevated volatility to earn income 
through structured strategies. In bonds, we prefer the 3-7-year 
part of the Developed Market bond yield curve and BBB-to-BB-
rated corporate bonds to earn attractive income as AI supply 
eases. In FX, we position for a modestly weaker USD over 6 -
12 months by allocating to ex-US assets.  
Policy implications:  Oil fell 12% over the past week as 
Democrats captured the US Senate and House. The party also 
flipped governorships in five states – Georgia, Iowa, Nevada, 
Ohio and Texas – securing a majority of governorships among 
50 US states for the first time since 2010. Vice President Vance 
and Republican leaders attributed the defeat to the Iran conflict, 
making it harder for President Trump to sustain it. Democrat 
control of the defence budget from next year further constrains 
conflict escalation. Despite near-term escalation risks, we see 
the geopolitical risk premium in oil prices fading, with oil easing 
towards USD 80/bbl  over 6-12 months. Oil-driven disinflation 
should allow central banks to adopt less aggressive policies. 
Adding tactical exposure to US IT sector : Semiconductors 
slumped this week amid concerns over data-centre moratoria, 
while beneficiaries of Democrat policies include defensives, 
affordability-related stocks, tariff rollback beneficiaries and  the 
healthcare sector. We use the volatility to add tactical exposure 
to the US IT sector . As we enter mid -cycle dynamics, equities 
should return 8-10% annually. The 2015–2017 mid-cycle offers 
a useful comparison: with an opposition Congress,  S&P 500 
returns slowed to 8.3% , but remained positive. Diversification 
across sectors and regions remains a priority amid the rotation. 
Prefer belly of the bond curve: Government bonds rallied on 
expectations of slower growth. We still expect two more Fed 
hikes by H1 2027, given sticky near-term inflation and a robust 
economy. However, cheaper oil should lower inflation towards 
the Fed’s 2% target next year, allowing rate cuts in H2 2027. 
Ultra-long yields remain elevated due to  fiscal challenges. 
Bonds, particularly those from non-hyperscaler investment -
grade corporates , offer attractive income. The 3 -7-year 
segment – the belly of the yield curve – offers the best balance 
of income and sensitivity to interest-rate changes. 
Credit to benefit from restrained AI supply:  Since states  
control data centre regulations, Democrat wins in governorship 
races in leading data centre states, such as Texas and Georgia, 
means growth in this segment is likely to be restricted. Slower 
data-centre construction could improve credit supply -demand 
conditions due to reduced issuance by hyperscalers. However, 
some data -centre-focused deals could become less viable, 
warranting caution towards lower -quality issuers. High -yield 
credit spreads have widened, but BB- and B-rated bonds have 
held up, while CCC -rated debt has r eached distressed levels. 
We prefer the BBB–BB range, spanning the boundary between 
investment-grade and high-yield debt, for income. 
A modest headwind for the USD, tailwind for gold: The 
dollar should remain range-bound near term before weakening 
modestly over 6-12 months. Democrat control of the Congress 
limits tariff escalation , fiscal expansion and rapid AI buildout , 
three pillars of US exceptionalism supporting the USD through 
2026. Disinflation should also encourage a less aggressive Fed 
stance, removing another source of USD support. Near -term 
support for the USD index (DXY) remains at 100.7 . A weaker-
than-expected payrolls report tonight could accelerate the 
USD’s decline, and vice versa . A softer dollar and sustained 
central-bank buying, led by China, remain supportive of gold.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions 
  What are the equity market implications of the US mid -
term election results?   
Our view: The US mid -term election outcome adds political 
headwinds but does not alter our outlook for positive equity 
returns. Investors should maintain a Core allocation to global 
equities and continue to diversify their equity exposure across 
regions and sectors. We initiate an opportunistic idea on the US 
information technology (IT) sector, moving our opportunistic 
idea positioning from a de fensive tilt to a more balanced 
positioning. Higher volatility also presents opportunities to use 
structured strategies for equity exposure.  
Rationale: The Democrats’ victory in both chambers of Congress 
has supported the outperformance of themes aligned with their 
policy priorities , with clean energy stocks , including solar 
companies, among the notable beneficiaries. Affordability-related 
themes have also benefited, including companies that could gain 
from potential tariff rollbacks. Medicaid-related stocks  have also 
risen on hopes that healthcare will gain from policy changes. 
Meanwhile, semiconductor stocks remain under pressure  amid 
concerns about potential moratoriums on data centres. Our AI capital 
expenditure (capex) estimates remain conservative relative to 
consensus, but with a potential reset in expectations around data 
centre spending, investors should remain selective.  
We initiate an opportunistic idea on the US IT sector after a 
significant 17% correction this quarter. Historically, this has been 
followed by strong positive returns over the next few quarters ( see 
table). Semiconductors, about 40% of US IT, appears oversold after 
correcting more than 30% since we closed our opportunistic idea a 
month ago. Earnings growth for US IT continues to be solid, with 
volume driven growth in semiconductors, AI monetisation 
opportunities, ongoing cloud growth and resilient services revenue.  
As we highlighted in our November 2026 Global Market Outlook , 
the transition to mid -cycle is typically characterised by greater 
political headwinds. The mid -term election outcome reinforces this 
trend. The mid-cycle experience of 2015-17 offers a useful historical 
parallel for returns that moderate, but remain positive. Although we 
trimmed global equities to a Core allocation last week, we continue 
to expect equities to deliver mid-to-high single-digit returns per 
annum during the mid -cycle, even amid political headwinds, 
supported by steady earnings growth in both 2027 and 2028. 
Stay invested and diversify, with Overweight allocations to the US 
and A xJ, which offer the strongest earnings growth. Sector 
diversification is key as we expect market leadership to broaden. We 
are Overweight the US IT, US healthcare, US financial, US materials, 
Europe ex-UK financial and Japan financial sectors. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist

Policy uncertainty and a change in the innovation 
cycle is consistent with our view of a mid-cycle 
transition, where equity returns moderate but 
remain positive

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are the key takeaways from Q3 2026 US earnings? 
Our view:  Q3 US earnings remain robust, but more modest 
earnings beats reinforce our view that equity markets are 
entering a mid-cycle phase. We remain Overweight US equities, 
but recommend investors to maintain diversified sector 
exposure as returns moderate and market leadership broadens. 
Rationale: As of 30 October, 82% of S&P500 companies that ha d 
reported had beaten earnings estimates. However, aggregate 
earnings were just 4.5% above expectations, a smaller beat than in 
recent quarters. Still, Q3 US earnings growth remain s robust at 
31.6% y/y, supporting expected full-year growth of 36.1% in 2026 
and 16.4% in 2027 for the S&P500. The slower pace of upgrades 
suggests the bull market is maturing  as investors focus on the 
sustainability of earnings growth. This raises the hurdle for positive 
share price reactions and makes selectivity more essential. For Big 
Tech, capex commitments remain high, while free cash flow is under 
pressure and expected to remain negative. Financing risks weigh on 
sentiment, particularly for companies with weaker balance sheets.  
We remain Overweight US equities , given the strong earnings 
growth, but we expect elevated volatility  and modest returns. We 
prefer quality exposure within technology  while maintaining  
diversified exposure across US financials, healthcare and materials. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  What to expect from Japanese banks’ upcoming results?   
Our view:  We expect  Japanese banks’  earnings growth  to 
continue over the medium term, supported by higher domestic 
net interest income (NII) and resilient fee income. We remain 
positive on the sector, favouring banks with strong deposit 
franchises and scope to increase shareholder returns. 
Investors will focus on whether full -year guidance upgrades 
and buybacks exceed expectations. 
Rationale: The BoJ’s September rate hike to 1.25% took effect late 
in the quarter, so its benefit should be more visible from the 
December quarter as loans reprice. We expect three further BoJ 
rate hikes by June 2027, supporting further NII expansion.  We 
will also look for signs that stronger corporate investment is driving 
faster loan growth and higher financing and advisory fees.  
We prefer to place more weight on guidance upgrades driven by 
recurring lending and fee income than on those driven by gains on 
equity disposals. Continued reductions in cross -shareholdings 
could free up capital for additional buybacks, providing a further 
boost to return on equity  (ROE). Key risks:  faster-than-expected 
deposit rate repricing, rising credit costs and bond portfolio losses. 
—  Robin Xie, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are the fundamental reasons behind your India 
equity upgrade? Which sectors offer favourable risk-reward? 
Our View: Indian equities offer an increasingly attractive risk -
reward profile. With India’s valuation premium to Emerging 
Markets (EMs) narrowing, foreign ownership near decade lows, 
Underweight EM positioning  and a stabilising Indian rupee 
(INR), conditions are increasingly supportive of Indian equity 
outperformance vs. the broader AxJ region.  
Rationale: We have upgraded Indian equities to Overweight within 
our AxJ equities allocation. While Indian markets  have witnessed 
USD 32bn of foreign investor outflows in 2026 year-to-date, strong 
domestic institutional investor inflows of USD 74bn  have more 
than offset foreign selling. These outflows and the Indian equity 
market’s underperformance vs . global peers reflect broader EM 
allocation shifts towards AI , rather than weakening India n 
fundamentals. Capital has gravitated toward s markets such as the 
US, Taiwan and  South Korea, which offer direct exposure to AI 
beneficiaries. However, we expect market leadership to broaden, as 
the rapid AI buildout is increasing ly debt-funded, and US mid-term 
election results raise uncertainty. We prefer to diversify and we see 
investors reallocating towards structurally strong  growth markets 
such as India, which offer non-AI driven growth.  
The RBI’s measures have supported INR stability,  but underlying 
USD demand remains elevated. Lower oil prices would improve 
external balances, but the INR is likely to follow a path of gradual, 
controlled depreciation rather than a quick downward move. On 
valuation to its own historical average, the Nifty 50 one-year forward 
P/E is trading 18% below its long-term average while ROE at 14.7% 
is above historical averages. With India now marginally Underweight 
in large EM portfolios compared to  the c.2% average Overweight 
historically, the case for a recovery in foreign flows is 
strengthening. 
India’s earnings growth remains intact . The Nifty 50 clocked Q1 
FY27 and Q2 FY27 profit-after-tax growth of c.20% y/y (ex -oil 
marketing companies) . Large -caps offer greater earnings visibility 
and downside protection, while stronger growth in small - and mid-
caps warrants a selective approach given elevated valuations.  
India’s growth outlook remains supported by a strengthening 
investment cycle, manufacturing momentum, supply chain 
diversification, resilient consumption and healthy corporate and 
banking balance sheets. Importantly, earnings growth remains 
robust and broa d-based across the financial, metals, telecom, 
automobiles and other domestic sectors, with Nifty 50 earnings 
growth expectations of c.14-15% over 2027 vis a vis A xJ’s 
technology driven earnings growth of 29% in 2027. 
—  Shaily Gang, Chief Investment Strategist, India

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Does the emerging US job market data alter your view of 
the Fed’s rate path and government bond yields? 
Our view: We do not expect the recent and incoming US job 
market data to alter our outlook unless there are significant 
deviations from the US economy’s current trajectory.  
Rationale: The US economy continues to exhibit resilience, with the 
latest Job Openings and Labour Turnover Survey data showing job 
openings surprising to the upside at 7.5mn. Initial jobless claims 
remain near three-month lows. There is little evidence of a material 
slowdown in labour demand. Inflation is expected to trend lower 
through H2 2027, but the disinflationary impulse remains a medium-
term story and is unlikely to manifest meaningfully in the near term. 
The market is now pricing in two additional 25bps hikes by H1 
2027 (down from three previously), in line with our view. These two 
rate hikes are largely priced into the US government bond yield 
curve. We estimate 2- and 10-year US government bond yields to 
range between 4.5-4.75% and 5-5.25%, respectively, over the next 
three months . Our twelve -month forecast for 2- and 10 -year US 
government bond yields are 4.25-4.5% and 4.75-5%, respectively, 
The 2-year yield is already trading within our expected range and 
would only move materially higher if a significantly stronger -than-
expected payrolls print led markets to price in a third hike. With two 
hikes baked in, long -run disinflation expectations intact and an 
uncertain fiscal outlook, the longer end of the  yield curve is 
expected to be less sensitive to a single payrolls release. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  What is your outlook for the GBP following the BoE’s rate 
hike this week? Do you see any impact on EUR/GBP? 
Our view: The BoE  rate hike supports  the GBP, but upside 
remains limited. We remain constructive on EUR/GBP, 
expecting a rebound towards 0.8450. 
Rationale: The BoE hiked by 25bps to 4% as expected . The GBP 
was little changed, despite hawkish inflation guidance. With markets 
pricing only around 50bps of additional BoE tightening over the next 
12 months, we see the GBP remaining vulnerable. In contrast, the 
ECB held its deposit rate at 2.50% in October but signalled readiness 
to tighten if inflation remains elevated. We expect Euro area inflation 
to rise further, keeping the ECB’s tightening bias alive. This suggests 
EUR/GBP has fallen too far relative to interest rate differentials.  
Germany’s planned increase in federal debt issuance  should 
support Euro area growth, while Moody’s decision not to downgrade 
France has removed an important EUR tail risk. Meanwhile, tighter 
UK fiscal policy is likely to weigh on domestic growth and business 
investment. We expect EUR/GBP to recover towards 0.8450. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

We expect 2- and 10-year US government bond 
yields to range between 4.5-4.75% and 5-5.25%, 
respectively, over the next three months 
US 2- and 10-year government bond yields and

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Why is the AUD underperforming despite the RBA hike? 
Our view: The AUD has lagged because markets had largely 
priced in the RBA’s November rate hike to 4.85%, and the bank 
gave investors less reason to expect another. Equity weakness 
also weighed on the currency. We remain bullish on AUD/USD  
and would add on pullbacks. Australia’s interest rate advantage 
and a sustained recovery in risk sentiment should support a 
move towards 0.72 in three months and 0.74 in 12 months. 
Rationale: Ahead of the 3 November RBA rate decision, market 
pricing implied an 88% chance of a 25bp s hike, leaving little scope 
for an upside surprise. What changed after the announcement 
was the pricing of the next hike.  The RBA dropped its reference 
to further rate increases, and three members preferred a rate hold. 
Markets scaled back the odds of rates reaching 5.10% next year to 
around 30%, thus weighing on the AUD. 
The AUD is sensitive to swings in global risk appetite, especially 
the US tech sell-off spread to global equities, triggering margin calls 
and weighing on  the currency. An equity recovery would ease that 
pressure. Even after the two 25bp s Fed hikes we expect by mid -
2027, Australia would retain a 35bps rate advantage  over the top 
of the Fed’s target range, supporting a gradual AUD recovery. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

We see AUD/USD upside risks on a sustained 
recovery in risk sentiment
